### Closure of the 182 upstream-only public APIs

The active goal covers every entry classified `upstream-only` in `coverage/api.json` at shipped `9a30600e`. All 182 keys have existing routes, including 159 callables and 23 classes. The dependency waves cover 4 W1, 8 W2, 35 W3, 49 W4, 21 W5 and 65 ecosystem entries. Each computational API needs an admitted contract, owned computation or an explicitly reviewed provider boundary and installed public and SDK evidence where applicable. Each advertised function needs a relevant fixed one-worker comparison. Stateful APIs require lifecycle and public-member evidence. Data, I/O, plotting and interactive APIs require their pinned asset, codec, plugin or protocol contracts. A partial registration leaves its uncovered domains open for this goal.

Root owns shared registry, build and workflow wiring, independent admission, the exclusive timing window and delivery. Freeze each contract and exact reference cases before dependent implementation. ### Phases and exit conditions

| Phase | Execute | Exit condition and reuse |
|---|---|---|
| 1. Reconcile once on resume | Read current source, existing receipts and unfinished packets; assign one owner per writable surface. | Name each packet's exact missing gate. Do not recapture references, rebuild products or replay tests just to refresh bookkeeping. |
| 2. Implement a coherent family | Freeze header/cases early. Implement native code and Python/SDK integration in parallel against that interface; include inventory routes, exported headers and dependency declarations in the same increment. | Changed-family parity passes, with scalar/SIMD, relevant worker modes and explicit providers. Reuse frozen original observations within their valid runtime/platform scope. |
| 3. Run the real setup barrier | Execute the COMPLETE actual workflow source-check command on both retained supported Python versions, with real selectors and normal conftest/plugins enabled; refuse real scientific imports as required by that stage. Run without fail-fast so every failure is visible. | Both complete executions pass. Collection audits, partial-run unions and altered test configuration cannot satisfy this release gate. After a fix, rerun the complete affected barrier before hosted release qualification; preserve all failures. Numerical/benchmark reuse remains separate and requires identical production dependencies. |
| 4. Freeze and prepare | Build a genuine product once for the intended source/profile. Combine already-ready compatible increments before freezing; do not delay indefinitely for an unfinished optimization. | Authentic product, installed family/SDK evidence and exact execution provenance. Reuse existing eligible products for measurements; preparation does not imply full qualification. |
| 5. Qualify and measure | Run one four-profile campaign for the frozen cumulative release candidate. Separately measure each ready family using genuine prepared artifacts on isolated hosts, with one worker and the existing sampler/gates. | Keep release qualification and scoped performance claims separate. A changed source needs new affected evidence; a failed setup is fixed before a successor campaign. Never rerun an unchanged performance experiment for favorable noise. |
| 6. Close promptly | Review once independently, merge/push the validated increment, update its existing record, then retire eligible worktrees. | Evidence links resolve, losses remain explicit, no completed source backlog. Preserve active packages, raw data, failed trials and protected paths. |

Family performance may proceed after genuine preparation and installed-family validation without waiting for the full cumulative campaign. It proves only the measured product/family, and cannot advance whole-product qualified coverage. The full release campaign remains mandatory for that claim.

### Reuse and rerun rules

| Input/change | Required work | Work to avoid |
|---|---|---|
| Documentation or receipt correction only | Link/format/scope checks; recompute affected reported values from retained raw data if necessary. | Numerical reruns, package builds, repeated downloads or full archive rehashing solely for a new administrative receipt. |
| Test/launcher/inventory change | Fix the failing context and affected consumers. Before hosted release qualification, run the complete actual source barrier on both retained supported Python versions with normal conftest/plugins, including after source-guard fixes. | Treating AST/collection or a union of partial runs as the full release barrier; rebuilding or remeasuring unchanged numerical production merely to correct metadata. |
| Native or frontend behavior change | Recompile affected translation units locally; rerun changed-family and dependent behavior tests, then prepare the exact genuine candidate for hosted claims. | New environments, full local SDK rebuilds, or whole-library campaigns for every private edit. |
| Shared ABI, runtime, compiler policy or dependency change | Expand validation to the affected dependency closure; full supported-profile qualification before release acceptance. | Reusing incompatible products or calling a partial receipt whole-product proof. |
| Immutable fixture/artifact | Decode once per appropriate test scope; authenticate once at receipt and reuse read-only content with its recorded digest and scope. | Per-case JSON parsing, repeated positive-envelope copies, duplicate downloads and independent reviewers rehashing unchanged multi-GB archives. |
| Mutated fixture, new trust boundary or platform-sensitive observation | Fresh validation of the changed object; independent mutable negative fixtures; narrow original witness only where the old reference is invalid. | Global validator caches that conceal mutations, normalization that forces agreement, or wholesale golden recapture. |

Use existing module/session fixtures for immutable test data. Keep mutation cases isolated and all assertions intact. Test-fixture deduplication is a small separate test-only packet; do not introduce a general production cache. Record observed test wall time and temporary bytes before/after from ordinary runs; do not claim estimated parsing traffic is physical I/O or proven saved time.

## Remaining work and completion gates (2026-11-04)

This is the live route to completion, alongside the execution/reuse policy above. Use the explicit next gates below for execution; plan maintenance itself launches no scientific jobs. Reconcile actual owners and handles before dispatch. Earlier checkpoint assignments are historical, including packet names still present in old operational fields.

### Account for all work, not just new API names

Use [the existing per-key routes](API_IMPLEMENTATION_PATHS.md), their [generated JSON](API_IMPLEMENTATION_PATHS.json), [the inventory](api.json), and [the task ledger](work_items.json). Do not duplicate 477 rows in another tracker. For each current packet identify public keys/member paths, uncovered contract partitions, native/provider ownership, prerequisites, affected SDK surface, parity command, benchmark or precise blocker, and next owner/gate. Attach new work to these records; never edit frozen observations to make a task appear finished.

Milestones describe dependency order, not global barriers: M1-M5 start when the relevant prerequisite is ready. P stays active throughout. Do not wait until all211 upstream-only names are registered to revisit partial domains, stateful APIs, providers or speed losses. Discovery targets the pinned release; a later upstream release is a separate scope decision, not an endless moving target.

### Make each selected route executable

Promote only the next selected per-key route into a bounded packet using the existing `owns`, `depends_on`, `contract.uncovered_cases`, `acceptance` and `next_gate` fields. Name the exact domains and callable/member paths, retained reference scope, native/provider boundary, affected SDK client, parity selectors, benchmark owner and terminal evidence. Do not re-audit all 477 proposals before starting one ready packet. A task blocked on a backend names that dependency; it does not disappear from the remaining work.

For each new kernel, record applicable SIMD/codegen evidence or a justified serial disposition, scalar parity, global budgets 1/4 and default restoration. Test changed nested/provider concurrency boundaries and reuse unchanged worker-policy proofs. Keep one-thread public-call timing mandatory; measure scaling separately. Prioritize retained speed losses by absolute latency and representative impact, alongside unmeasured canonical APIs, rather than optimizing the win ratio.

Run representative hot-swap compositions as each family becomes available, before M6: include cross-family dtype/layout transfer and, when relevant, callbacks, classes, lazy results and provider availability. Begin one runtime-object lifecycle partition now from saved RegionProperties discovery, covering construction failures, properties, methods, cache/mutation and generated attributes. Static-member inventories alone do not close these contracts. Extend existing tests and clients instead of creating a parallel test framework.

### Executable route for each selected API

Promote only the next dependency-ready partition from the existing per-key easiest-path proposal. Keep native, provider, unassessed and unmeasured domains explicit in its existing contract/acceptance fields; a registration or benchmark link does not close an API.

1. **Contract first:** inspect pinned source, specify dtype/layout/axis/output/error/state partitions and safe original inputs. Reuse valid saved observations; capture only missing observations after safety review.
2. **Smallest implementation:** aliases reuse their canonical kernel with namespace/identity checks; compositions reuse accepted primitives. Direct loops start scalar, then vectorize independent spans. Neighborhood/connectivity primitives ship with their first concrete consumer. Classes start with constructor/lifecycle/mutation; numerical backends start pinned with one real consumer.
3. **Parallel ownership:** freeze the typed interface; split native, Python parity, and SDK/benchmark files. Give shared registry/CMake/qualifier wiring to one integration owner. Each function/member has declared parity tests; numerical fallback is denied for owned-native claims.
4. **Actual setup closure:** inspect all literal file/hash assertions and transitive read dependencies in the actual workflow selectors, including generic checks, wildcard imports and package/SDK census fixtures. Before hosted release qualification, execute the complete actual command on both retained supported interpreters with normal conftest/plugins and real scientific imports refused, without fail-fast. Fix all failures and rerun the complete affected barrier. Collection, partial unions and altered configuration are not this gate; unchanged production proofs remain reusable separately.
5. **Product and speed:** prepare a coherent exact-source product once; exercise installed SDK clients and allocation-inclusive one-worker original comparisons. Fix old-C++ holdouts before optimization; retain every loss. Measure optional global-worker scaling separately. Qualify cumulative release checkpoints on all four profiles rather than every private edit.
6. **Close and select:** independent acceptance, waste review, affected next-action correction, merge/push and safe retirement. Choose the next missing API, uncovered partial contract, and bottleneck/discovery packet from existing routes; do not start work solely to fill a slot.

For M3/P, B's next stateful packet selects one genuinely unassessed RegionProperties property/lifecycle partition from the remaining35 unassessed properties, after the captured area_bbox/extent partition, using saved discovery to name its exact property, dependencies and parity selector before implementation. Do not repeat the already-covered seven-property label/slice/input-mutation work. If its primitive is unavailable, record that prerequisite and select another ready partition; SDK high-level behavior remains required.

A packet may terminate accepted, source-ready with named remaining gates, failed/discarded with retained evidence, or blocked on an explicit prerequisite. Full completion still requires every final-acceptance row, including partial domains, classes/runtime discovery and performance dispositions.

### Three execution lanes, bounded work in progress

Keep the current frozen release on its own delivery path. While its hosted qualification runs, three workers may develop the next independent coherent batches in separate branches without modifying the running candidate or delaying its delivery. Root owns shared registry, build, workflow and release wiring. Assign each worker family-specific source/header/wrapper/test files before editing; workers must preserve others' changes. Each worker delivers its whole vertical slice rather than splitting one small API across three serial handoffs. These are planned assignments, not running agents.

| Lane | Next bounded batch and easiest path | Boundary and exit |
|---|---|---|
| A / polygon fill | Reuse the already-reviewed draw.polygon and draw.polygon2mask components; separate them from rejected captured-key experiment ancestry. | Inspect existing packets and source projections before editing. Reuse scanline/inclusion and mask scatter; close missing public/SDK/parity/performance gates rather than reimplementing proved code. |
| B / Bezier coordinates | draw.bezier_curve using a source-faithful native rational recurrence and the existing line leaf for its literal fallback. | Border assessment found both polygon_perimeter and rectangle_perimeter always invoke Matplotlib clipping, absent from retained runtimes; keep those APIs open behind pinned clipping/reference admission. Use the independent Bezier leaf now. Freeze safe finite branch cases, preserving rational weights, extrema, order and errors; an initial unit-weight subset cannot close the full contract. |
| C / color scatter | draw.set_color: native scatter/blend with public in-place dtype, alpha and repeated-index semantics. | Inspection found no existing native contingency/label-pair-count implementation in src/include/python, so use the already-planned smaller fallback. Freeze boundary and mutation cases before implementation; reuse worker/runtime and conversion machinery. Keep the three label-pair metric routes open for a later coherent leaf, without introducing a general sparse backend now. |

Merge/push qualified increments promptly. Do not hold a ready bundle for an unfinished third lane. |

Each existing packet must name its exact public contract, writable files, affected selectors, installed client and benchmark cases. Reuse one family case table and the existing sampler; do not create a controller per API. Every function still needs parity and a relevant original comparison, with scalar/SIMD and optional global-worker behavior assessed. Ordered operations may have a justified serial core; default workers remain1. No native-ownership credit for provider computation.

After these batches, rotate one lane through uncovered partial/class/SDK contracts and demonstrated performance losses so easy registrations do not starve contract completion. Select from the existing477 routes, not a second backlog. Independently review author evidence without repeating unchanged numerical proofs. A new binary still requires exact-source genuine preparation and the mandated supported-profile release campaign.

Current ownership follows the terminal evidence: root monitors exact334 campaign42140207750 and owns release/publication. The polygon/Bezier/set_color and Prescott source authorship is frozen; neither completed benchmark nor faileddb4 qualification is a dispatch order. A's private label-pair counts064/2959 packet has independent C peer dd3db80 after the flags-only scalar correction; public/reducer policy work remains separate. B's metadata-only release fix and documentation are closed; new polygon optimization is a source-only proposal, not coding/timing GO. ### Rolling selection after the current cumulative checkpoint

At each accepted checkpoint, choose the next three non-overlapping packets from the existing routes: (A) the smallest dependency-ready missing API/family, (B) an uncovered dtype/layout/state/SDK or hot-swap partition, and (C) a measured bottleneck or pinned-release discovery partition. Prefer work that unlocks an existing consumer; share a primitive only when its consumers are concrete. If a lane depends on unfinished code, assign independent ready work or leave it idle instead of creating speculative tooling. Freeze interfaces and reference cases before dependent implementation; explicitly assign one shared integration owner, with root retaining admission/main delivery. Rotate independent reviews so nobody accepts their own changes.

For every selected API, turn its easiest-route entry into exact inputs/outputs, native/provider boundaries, affected selectors, installed client and benchmark cases in its existing packet. Start with source-faithful scalar correctness, vectorize independent arithmetic/spans, and parallelize only independent work behind the global budget. Alias/composition routes reuse existing kernels; stateful routes add lifecycle/mutation cases before broader solvers; backend routes start with one real consumer. No calendar estimate or API-count target replaces the declared acceptance gates.

A packet exits only when its affected evidence is reviewed, its remaining gaps are explicit, and its closeout has updated the next action. A failed experiment exits as failed with the next demonstrated cause to test; it does not stay in the active dispatch queue. At each release checkpoint, reconcile the existing inventory/roadmap against accepted contract partitions, performance dispositions and runtime discovery; do not regenerate scientific observations to refresh status. Stop declaring progress at native-partial counts: final completion requires every row below.

### Route from this checkpoint to full completion

1. **Close the existing measured increment.** Independently admit retained coords_scaled performance, run its exact-source four-profile qualification once, deliver the reviewed source, then clear only eligible obsolete worktrees/caches. If a gate fails, preserve the run and fix its exact cause before a successor; do not reopen passing evidence without an input change.
2. **Deliver independent API batches.** Use the polygon fill, polygon border and label-pair metric lanes above, with the explicit smaller fallback for lane C. Reuse proved components and one shared integration owner. Keep34 unassessed runtime properties and the remaining29-class/284-member scope visible.
3. **Drain dependency-ready routes continuously.** At each packet transition, select A's next missing computational key, B's next uncovered partial/state/SDK contract, and C's measured bottleneck or discovery gap from the existing per-key routes. Do not materialize hundreds of guessed tasks in advance. Expand only the selected route into exact inputs, files, dependency, parity selector, installed client and benchmark/holdout; use M1-M5's easiest routes above. Alias/composition work reuses kernels, neighborhood consumers reuse established ordering primitives, and backend work begins with one concrete consumer.
4. **Close contracts as well as registrations.** For each477-key/member record, assess remaining dtype/layout/dimension/output/error/state/provider domains against the pinned release. Every uncovered partition gets an owner and prerequisite; a native-partial registration cannot close the key. Keep runtime discovery and ecosystem contracts active throughout. Extend required high-level SDK behavior rather than counting a primitive export as class coverage.
5. **Drain performance losses with demonstrated mechanisms.** Use existing one-worker raw comparisons to rank representative absolute cost, conversion/allocation overhead and prior-C++ regressions. Freeze targets and holdouts before source changes; retain every rejected experiment. Add SIMD only to independent arithmetic/spans and optional parallelism only where measurements justify it, using the process-global budget/default1. A remaining speed target requires an explicit disposition, never an average that conceals a loss.
6. **Freeze and qualify the complete product.** Once all declared partitions and discovery boundaries close, validate backend-swapped pipelines, installed SDK and required scalar/SIMD/worker modes, then run the final exact-source four-profile campaign. Check every acceptance row below, publish limitations and retained benchmark evidence, merge/push and clean only released eligible work. No completion claim while a required contract, numerical provider, performance target or supported profile remains unresolved.

At every numbered step, task closeout is part of that task: result/source/evidence; avoidable work and reusable/pending evidence; corrected next owner/trigger or **reviewed, no change**. Root reviews it before the next dependent task. Unrelated ready work continues. A repeated setup error changes the next setup barrier; a scientific rejection changes the next hypothesis; a metadata-only correction changes only publication checks. Do not add a second ledger, repeated broad audit or a scientific rerun to satisfy this review.

### Final acceptance matrix

| Dimension | Required evidence before declaring complete |
|---|---|
| Inventory and ownership | All 477 current keys, 29 classes/284 static members and newly discovered pinned-release paths accounted for; aliases/metadata/assets separately classified. All required computation is owned C++ or a declared reviewed native backend. Partial scopes and numerical forwarding are open gaps. |
| Hot-swappable Python | Same public imports/signatures/defaults, aliases, output types/dtypes/layout/ownership, warnings/errors and observable callbacks/state. Run representative complete pipelines with either backend, including classes, lazy objects and optional-provider availability. Every API/member has contract tests; a registry smoke test is insufficient. |
| Installed C++ | Self-contained installed headers and relocated downstream clients call every exposed computational family and the required high-level behavior without Python algorithm providers. Primitive-only SDK support is labeled partial until required high-level composition/state is covered. |
| CPU and workers | Portable scalar and dispatched SIMD parity for applicable paths; default one worker and optional global budgets verified, including nested/provider use. Benchmark small-call overhead and scaling separately. Serial ordered cores are justified explicitly; do not force unsafe parallelism or claim every operation vectorizes. |
| Per-API performance | Every relevant canonical computational API has frozen representative one-worker public-call comparisons with original, genuine source/product/runtime identity, raw samples and slower cases. New optimizations also pass prospective prior-C++ controls. Targets/holdouts are fixed before candidate results; aliases reuse the kernel benchmark plus path-overhead checks. Metadata/I/O/state use relevant latency/cache behavior, not invented image throughput. |
| Performance disposition | Close each demonstrated bottleneck with a tested mechanism. Rank work by absolute cost and representative impact. If a target remains unattainable, retain the failed evidence and obtain an explicit scope/target decision; no quiet waiver or aggregate average can turn a loss into completion. |
| Release and delivery | All four supported profiles qualify the same final frozen source with actual public/SDK/corpus results. Publish source, build/install/usage instructions and performance limitations; merge/push accepted work and retire eligible worktrees under AGENTS.md. Earlier accepted products never qualify later code by implication. |

Obtain scientific interpreter paths from successful retained receipts, verify the required scientific dependencies/source pins, and exercise every prospective capture ID with originals refused before capture. Build-directory names are not runtime identities. Before calling originals for a new family, inspect source-visible unsafe indexing, overflow and allocation preconditions. Classify known upstream-unsafe inputs separately before capture; preserve failed/partial journals and resume only uncalled safe IDs. Never replay completed original captures or manufacture expected results for an unsafe path.

For cross-host preparation/verification, pin the same exact interpreter build policy on both jobs before dispatch. Floating minor-version selectors can resolve to different patches even in one campaign. A demonstrated interpreter input change requires a new prospective preparation; preserve the failed run and never weaken runtime identity to make it pass. Read hash conventions explicitly (raw bytes versus canonical source text) before review; use explicit UTF-8 and absolute retained interpreter paths in worktrees.

Before narrowing a contract or adding a defensive path for a suspected numerical mismatch, verify actual operation semantics with a source-safe bounded witness. Nominal dtype/result_type inference alone can be wrong for ufunc comparisons. Keep rejected hypotheses and failed diagnostics, and restore the unchanged implementation when evidence disproves the need for a guard.

Finalize path normalization, labels and the task closeout before handing off a receipt hash. Once handed off, keep those bytes immutable; issue a versioned successor for corrections and retain the predecessor. A metadata correction changes only affected publication bytes/review, never triggers a scientific replay.

### Review waste after every task

A task is a bounded implementation, reference, review, setup, qualification, measurement or publication packet, not each test or sample. At every terminal outcome (accepted, failed, discarded, or handed off as blocked), its owner adds the following short closeout to the existing handoff/current checkpoint. Root reviews it before starting the next dependent task. Close a failed or discarded packet as well as an accepted one; do not leave its former dispatch action live.

```text
Result / source / evidence: <terminal outcome and exact source/receipt; distinguish science from setup failure>
Waste and reuse: <avoidable repetition/setup/drift; observed cost or unknown; reusable and still-pending evidence>
Next-plan change: <update the affected live next gate with owner/trigger and remove obsolete dispatch; or reviewed, no change>
```

Keep this to a few lines. No fresh timing, reference capture, artifact download, test replay or environment is needed to fill it. A correctly rejected scientific candidate is useful evidence, not automatically waste. Derive reported counts from retained XML/journals; unknown costs stay unknown. Closeout belongs to the task itself, not an extra audit project. If no correction is needed, continue without a standalone documentation commit.

For new/current packets, include this closeout requirement in their existing `acceptance` text and record details in their existing handoff or mutable `current_checkpoint`. Root updates the affected `next_gate`/`next_action` and the relevant phase/owner/rerun rule in this plan when a lesson changes execution. Do not invent another schema/validator or rewrite historical manifests. The existing `--packet` command exposes acceptance, but does not display all checkpoint/next-gate details; read the linked record when needed.

Before closing each task, answer four practical questions: did it advance a stated acceptance gate; was any capture/build/review duplicated; what can the next task reuse; and what specific dependency, owner or command must change now? Record only demonstrated problems. Separate unavoidable validation and useful rejected experiments from preventable setup failures. Resolve a changed prerequisite before dependent work; keep unrelated ready work moving. Do not start a new audit simply to fill this closeout.

If no plan change is warranted, record **reviewed, no change** in the existing task closeout; avoid a documentation-only commit for that entry alone. Reusable cross-project lessons go into the existing compilexx scikit-imagexx note only when there is a new lesson, not after every task. Include material plan changes in the same validated increment rather than deferring them to a periodic retrospective.

| Waste signal | Immediate adjustment before the next expensive task |
|---|---|
| Completed agent receives only a queued message | Check the actual roster at handoff. Use `followup_task` to start completed agents; a message alone does not resume execution. |
| Ready hosted work waits for broad ledger/publication or next-family review | Update its one dependent next gate and dispatch immediately once mandatory readiness is closed. Batch unrelated publication and queue refresh while the job runs; measure scheduling interval separately from useful task time, never claim it all as saved time. |
| Fresh tooling stage accidentally uses installed scientific packages | Run the actual source-only selectors with real scientific imports refused; retain runtime-dependent checks after their declared install stage. Do not add early dependencies solely to hide an import leak. |
| Generic fixture breaks on a new dtype or structured result | Exercise that changed result kind through existing generic dtype/alias consumers on both retained profiles with eligible cores before hosted preparation. Fix the boundary once; reuse passing cases. |
| Raw-byte-pinned fixture fails only on fresh Windows checkout | Reproduce actual Git conversion with core.autocrlf=true; mark the demonstrated exact-byte input -text, preserve its raw data/hash and rerun only the failing consumer. Do not weaken the hash or normalize historical evidence to make it pass. |
| Same class of setup failure repeats | Stop another expensive dispatch; fix and test the actual failing entry point and affected consumers. Do not build a generic framework to handle a narrow launcher error. |
| Guessed runtime/selector or stale closure counter | Resolve paths from successful receipts and actual manifests; verify scientific dependencies and semantic selector boundaries. Use the current benchmark hardware-metadata template. A narrow metadata failure reruns only its affected nodes. |
| Cross-Python metadata failure after source plans passed | Run changed source-only metadata selectors on both retained interpreters. Compare source bytes or same-interpreter parsed baselines, not a frozen AST-schema digest across versions. Preserve the failed campaign; no numerical tolerance or historical observation changes. |
| Repeated or oversized evidence publication | Retain raw payloads once with hashes and locators; publish bounded summaries and decision inputs. Reuse authenticated immutable receipts; do not duplicate full journals/runtime maps or introduce another reporting framework. |
| Proposed duplicate build/download/reference capture | State the changed source/dependency, invalid artifact or missing receipt that requires it; otherwise reuse the existing object. |
| Stale routes/counts/SDK packaging | Before the next expensive header/scope increment, enumerate current-stager/corpus fixtures from the actual setup selector and update their strict census/negative cases alongside the generator and consumers. Reuse passing nodes; rerun only affected failures when selection/isolation is unchanged. Do not repair old raw results or infer acceptance from counts. |
| Repeated immutable fixture work | Use the existing appropriate scoped fixture; keep mutated negative cases fresh and all assertions intact. Demonstrate savings only from ordinary subsequent runs. |
| Ready source waits on unrelated work or accumulating worktrees | Integrate the validated increment and schedule an exact-source checkpoint; safely retire only released clean ancestors, preserving proofs. |
| A supposed optimization only shifts cost into conversion/allocation/provider work | Keep the allocation-inclusive public benchmark and prior-C++ controls; investigate the demonstrated boundary cost before another candidate. |

**Kept float32 tiled streaming:** [the fixed12 one-thread comparison](../benchmarks/inertia-f32-streaming-same-host.md) gives **1.119084834x direct / 1.122603981x normalized gain** over the kept double-streaming product. The user's latest direction is tests first, then small implementation groups,
isolated one-thread benchmarks, and full supported-profile acceptance. Use the
[fast-run guide](FAST_RUN.md) for executable commands. The initial Windows
development smoke gate passed 1,286 checks: all 477 API surfaces, 206 registered
function signatures, 602 supported-dtype parity cases and one registry check.
That phase-1 run used the retained public203 package without rebuilding.
Phase 2 has integrated footprint shapes, colocalization, Butterworth/LPI/window filters, moments and derived moments, inertia tensors/eigenvalues, coordinate moments, Shannon entropy and default block sums, plus the partial `LPIFilter2D` class. RGB storage and packed uint8 optimizations extend existing APIs.
Current source coverage is 234 partial image APIs + 3 metadata functions (237 total), five partial classes and 234 upstream-only entries. The footprint component
contract passes 240 checks in normal and scalar modes, including two large
ellipse cases above the optional parallel-fill threshold. Inventory/runner
checks bring the normal scoped run to 735 passing tests; the five-feature
shared smoke also passes 26 checks. The CMake portable configuration verifies
strict FP and worker-refusal test wiring without rebuilding the whole product.
Colocalization's 545 public component checks pass in normal and scalar modes;
its normal run with inventory/runner checks passes 1,040 tests, and its shared
smoke passes 16. Its 1,608 original semantic projections are now a self-contained
224 KB compressed test fixture, checked against the existing frozen matrix.
AVX2 and portable CMake configurations confirm baseline/leaf ISA separation,
strict FP and optional-worker test wiring. Accepted combined binaries remain
public203; combined wheel/SDK qualification for the additions is pending.
One-thread performance results are now available below. Existing full contracts retain their scope; older
campaign priorities below do not supersede this phased route.

**Coordinate moments are integrated:** `measure.moments_coords` and `moments_coords_central` add owned float32/float64 monomial products and ordered point reductions, with SIMD across independent outputs and the existing global worker budget. NumPy tuple/center/cast/power preparation and K1/complex/exceptional reductions remain explicit providers. The 48 original-first specifications pass **1,940 family and 176 shared checks** across both profiles and dispatch modes; old records and all 716 archives remain exact. Source `a1dafca` is merged as `d1b6198`. [Genuine minimum Release40394363351](https://github.com/bipxx/scikit-imagexx/actions/runs/40394363351) passes **2,622 public checks and 1,373 smoke checks**. Its [first one-thread comparison](../benchmarks/moments-coords-minimum-initial.md) has **12/12 wins**, geometric mean **2.273374651x** (raw 2.712598165x; central 1.905270147x), retaining all 168 samples. Inputs are actual 256/1024-by-2 point clouds; size means points, not square images. Only a provider NaN-sign cross-worker assertion changed after saved originals demonstrated that sign instability; finite values, signed zeros, nonfinite masks, errors and state remain exact, with raw words/failure preserved. Native normal/scalar/sanitizer and separate portable/no-OpenMP controls pass. At the coordinate checkpoint, source coverage was **225 native-partial image APIs + 3 metadata functions = 228 registered functions**, with **243 upstream-only entries**. Full supported-profile qualification remains **203 image APIs**. The later Shannon integration is described above.

`util.unique_rows` is now publicly wired: 52 frozen cases, six native modes, 428 private pipeline checks and 460 bounded public/default checks pass. The one-thread nine-case measurement is running at [Release40695006125](https://github.com/bipxx/scikit-imagexx/actions/runs/40695006125); no UniqueRows speed claim is established. Byte-row ordering/equality/first indices are owned C++; contiguous preparation and final NumPy gather remain explicit providers. Source coverage is 241 image APIs, with full qualification still 203. The [LabelPoints baseline](../benchmarks/label-points-minimum-initial.md) retains all nine cases and 126 samples: 1/9 wins, geometric mean 0.802491952x, with all eight losses published. One fused bounds/offset/scatter candidate is under the fixed same-host comparison [40696160717](https://github.com/bipxx/scikit-imagexx/actions/runs/40696160717); no optimization gain is claimed yet.

**Kept large-double tiled streaming:** [the fixed12 controlled comparison](../benchmarks/inertia-streaming-same-host.md) gives **1.110031361x direct / 1.137751467x normalized gain** over the kept K=3 version. The four targeted 1024-square uint8/float64 cases improve **1.49886244x / 1.52056178x**, each repeating against both baselines under both metrics. Ten of twelve cases beat scikit-image in both candidate passes. All **672 samples**, **six direct/four normalized losses**, and the eight controls' 0.95526086x direct result remain published. Large float32 tensor/eigenvalue controls add 59.339/57.945 microseconds versus baseline and have mixed original-library outcomes; no cause is assigned to those regressions. Exact measured source `64b7fca` is merged as `cbf3050`, with 29 lines preserving reduction order, bounds and worker rules. Native output/FP streams match in eight modes, and 2,256 public checks retain all 2,224 old numerical rows exactly. Genuine preparation passes nine contract pairs and 1,365 smoke checks. This measured wheel predates the independent coordinate API integration; full supported-profile qualification stays 203 image APIs.

**Combined tensor/eigenvalue measurement:** [the 12-case controlled campaign](../benchmarks/inertia-pipeline-k3-same-host.md) verifies the actual combined `070acc7` product against pre-K3 `812a870`: **3.150593082x direct / 3.137168950x normalized gain**, with every case beating both baseline passes under both metrics. Tensor and eigenvalue groups improve **3.299412022x / 3.008486573x directly**. All **672 samples** and all rows are published. Eight of twelve cases beat scikit-image; four 1024-square uint8/float64 cases remain slower, including their absolute penalties in the report. Balanced original/current scores move 0.366327875x to 1.149232434x on one EPYC 9V74 host. Combined validation passes 1,548 component and 176 shared checks with unchanged saved arrays; genuine preparation passes nine normal/scalar contract pairs and 1,365 smoke checks. Those four residual losses motivated the separately measured and kept tiled-streaming experiment above; this first combined report remains unchanged. Full supported-profile qualification remains 203 image APIs.

**Kept K=3 projection optimization:** [the fixed six-case controlled comparison](../benchmarks/inertia-k3-same-host.md) gives **2.415797494x direct / 2.387212955x normalized gain** over the previous C++ inertia path. The three 1024-square targets improve **4.442269025x / 4.379652768x**, each repeating against both baseline passes under both metrics. All six cases beat scikit-image in both candidate passes; there are no direct or normalized regressions in this cohort. All **336 raw samples** are published. Source `a60df8e` is merged as `046f406`; it adds K=3 to the existing fused SIMD template with no formula, reduction-order, worker or tolerance changes. Eight focused kept/candidate native runs agree byte-for-byte on outputs/FP flags; 1,980 public checks and all 1,952 retained numerical records agree with baseline. Genuine preparation passes eight normal/scalar pairs and 1,361 smoke nodes without repeating standalone timings. The [first mixed benchmark](../benchmarks/inertia-tensor-minimum-initial.md) remains intact. This one-host measured wheel predates the independent eigenvalue integration; it is not a combined-main or full supported-profile qualification.

**Inertia eigenvalues are integrated:** `measure.inertia_tensor_eigvals` reuses the existing C++ symmetric solver through a 31-line binding and a 17-line frontend. Supplied T takes precedence; lower-triangle interpretation, clipping, descending scalar-list output and dtype/error behavior are covered by 34 original-first cases on both runtimes. Component validation passes **1,548 checks plus 88 shared checks**, with all prior tensor/moment observations and arrays unchanged; both profiles collect **1,365 smoke nodes**. The generic metadata caller validates this new API as a whole list before comparing item metadata, retaining the full context required by the frozen solver-plus-Weyl bound. Source `812a870` is merged as `d54f664`; [Genuine Release40383375851](https://github.com/bipxx/scikit-imagexx/actions/runs/40383375851) passes all nine installed contract pairs (2,426 checks) and 1,365 smoke checks. Its [first six-case benchmark](../benchmarks/inertia-eigvals-minimum-initial.md), before K=3 fusion, has one win and five losses (0.393826x geometric mean); all 84 raw samples remain published. The completed combined-product preparation40386034670 and 12-case controlled campaign above now measure the eigenvalue gain independently; its four remaining original-library losses stay visible. At that eigenvalue checkpoint source coverage was **223 native-partial image APIs + 3 metadata functions = 226 registered functions**, with **245 upstream-only entries**. Full supported-profile qualification stays **203 image APIs**. NumPy preparation, clipping, list wrapping and unsupported solver domains remain explicit providers.

**Inertia tensor is integrated:** `measure.inertia_tensor` adds one native-partial API, reusing owned central moments and a small typed C++ tensor assembly. NumPy indexing, diagonal summation and exceptional/integer/complex tensor stages remain explicit providers. The 57 original-first cases pass 1,272 component checks across both profiles and dispatch modes, plus 88 shared checks; old moments records and arrays match exactly. Source `7456bfd` is merged as `37b296e`. The first installed run exposed an unnecessary FFT storage check on a valid 3D moment tensor; removing six NumPy-stage checks fixed it without changing arithmetic, fixtures or bounds. [Genuine minimum Release](https://github.com/bipxx/scikit-imagexx/actions/runs/40378736391) passes all eight installed contract pairs, older regressions and 1,361 smoke checks. Its [first one-thread comparison](../benchmarks/inertia-tensor-minimum-initial.md) retains all 84 samples: three of six wins, geometric mean 0.627507734x original/current. All 256-square cases win; all 1024-square cases lose (+5.379077 ms uint8, +0.955479 ms float32, +5.322718 ms float64). Those initial losses motivated the separately kept K=3 optimization above; the original first-run data remain unchanged. At that tensor checkpoint source coverage was **222 native-partial image APIs + 3 metadata functions = 225 registered functions**, with **246 upstream-only entries**. Supported-profile qualification stays **203 image APIs**. The subsequent eigenvalue integration is described above.

**Kept C/F layout optimization:** [the fixed 13-case one-thread comparison](../benchmarks/moments-normalized-layouts-same-host.md) gives **3.346642894x direct / 3.338426256x normalized gain** over the kept C-only version. The four real F-layout targets improve **50.841838826x / 51.004018874x**, with both candidate passes beating both baselines; all 13 cases beat scikit-image in both candidate passes. **Kept normalized-moments batch optimization:** [the controlled one-thread comparison](../benchmarks/moments-normalized-batch-same-host.md) measures **51.483161594x float32 / 38.987071152x float64 gain over the previous C++ path**. Across all five cases the direct gain is **4.478043258x**, reference-normalized gain **4.649099324x**; every candidate case beats scikit-image in both passes on one EPYC 7763 host. All three Hu direct losses remain recorded, including float32 +0.221164 microseconds and losses in both metrics. The bounded default 4x4 C-contiguous real path performs powers and SIMD/scalar division in one native call; other layouts/options retain the tested scalar path. Source `aecf521` is merged as `f2eee88` with identical production code. Validation retains 4,819 native controls, 4,276 component/shared passes and 64 additional accepted/fallback checks; None probes have no arithmetic credit. Full qualification stays 203. That C-only checkpoint exposed the F-layout gap addressed by the separate kept 13-case experiment above. No whole-image pipeline speedup is claimed.

**Derived moments source integration adds `measure.moments_normalized` and `moments_hu`.** The 76 prospective specifications produce 155 nodes per runtime/dispatch mode: **620 new checks plus 3,408 unchanged-family checks = 4,028 component passes**. Actual shared-source validation adds 248 parity/error checks and 998 metadata checks; the selector collects 1,357 smoke nodes without executing unrelated tiny-core families. Hu owns the typed C++ polynomial and passes 1,518 native controls in both normal and sanitizer builds. The initial normalization path owns its final typed scalar division, retaining NumPy scalar providers; the optimized C-contiguous default path above now owns the batched arithmetic. A GCC scheduling bug that hid 13 zero-mass warnings is fixed by materializing the real quotient before reading FP status. Both APIs preserve the frozen outcomes and remain native-partial. Their five distinct 4x4 benchmark cases are prepared outside timing; integer normalization has exact source-error coverage and no numerical or timing credit. Source is merged/pushed as `76cdd70`; [the genuine minimum Release](https://github.com/bipxx/scikit-imagexx/actions/runs/40350415600) passes both installed contract modes and all 1,357 smoke checks. Its [first original-library comparison](../benchmarks/moments-derived-minimum-initial.md) retains all 70 samples: Hu wins 3/3 cases (1.09250x/1.33762x/1.28224x); normalization loses both (0.400917x float32, 0.290620x float64), adding 68.814/59.928 microseconds. Overall geometric mean is 0.737598829x. Those first losses motivated the kept batch path above. The later C/F optimization above admits bounded default F-layout inputs too. Full supported-profile qualification remains 203 image APIs. The 2,924-per-mode phase3 public barrier is configured, not yet an executed qualification. ## Decide the contract before choosing the implementation

For each API row, freeze a small contract matrix before inspecting new candidate
outputs. Cover the pinned signature/defaults, dtype and promotion, dimensions,
channel/axis semantics, memory layout and overlap, allocation/view behavior,
mutation, warnings/errors, callbacks, exceptional values, and provider calls.
Select cases by behavioral partitions and boundary conditions, not by maximizing
assertion counts. Add the relevant upstream tests and generated adversarial
cases; record unassessed partitions explicitly. A common registry smoke test is
necessary but does not replace this matrix.

Use the least complex route that satisfies the complete contract:

1. Reuse an already accepted canonical implementation for true aliases, while
   checking each public path independently.
2. Compose existing native primitives when their dtype, boundary, ordering, and
   ownership rules match. Do not introduce another kernel or provider adapter
   just to obtain a new API name.
3. Port a simple scalar C++ implementation of the reference algorithm where a
   primitive is missing. Establish finite, empty, exceptional, strided, and
   mutable-input behavior before adding fast paths.
4. Add SIMD and optional parallel work to independently computable hot loops.
   Preserve scalar dispatch and the existing global worker setting. Measure the
   public call, including conversions and allocation, before tuning further.
5. For FFT, sparse/dense linear algebra, and codecs, evaluate a suitable C++
   library behind a repository-owned contract instead of recreating a numerical
   ecosystem. Pin and document its build and threading behavior. A call to the
   original scikit-image algorithm, or a NumPy/SciPy algorithm provider, remains
   an explicit native gap. Genuine external protocols such as user callbacks,
   custom estimators, and codec plugins must retain their defined integration
   behavior without being counted as native solvers.

### Numerical parity policy

Structural behavior is exact: public routing, signature, shape, dtype/metadata,
container structure, view and mutation semantics, labels, parent/traverser
identities, stable ties, state transitions, warnings/errors, and callback order.
Byte-preserving copies/rearrangements and integer/discrete algorithms retain
their exact values. Preserve sign bits, NaN behavior, and arithmetic ordering
where they are observable in the advertised contract or already required by an
accepted strict proof.

For floating arithmetic, each contract chooses exactness or a justified
absolute/relative/ULP or residual-based comparison in advance. Use dtype,
conditioning, reference guarantees, and the algorithm's error propagation to
justify that choice. A generic `allclose` threshold is not a contract. Existing
strict proofs remain valid and unchanged; any different future numerical policy
is a separately reviewed prospective contract, with independent cases that were
not used to select it. Do not widen tolerances after looking at failures, adapt
to an oracle result, dispatch on fixture identities, or claim a provider result
as a C++ result.

Iterative algorithms need more than a final value check. Li callbacks, TV
stopping, reconstruction ordering, local optima, threshold ties, and random/model
solvers can amplify small differences. Check observable iteration/callback
behavior, convergence/failure semantics, downstream masks or labels, and
conditioning/near-boundary cases. Retain source-faithful ordering where those
semantics require it. CPU-specific bitwise imitation is justified only by a
required observable contract; it is not the default prerequisite for adding an
unrelated API.

## Three workers and one integrator

On resume, reconcile the frozen checkpoints first, then use the same three
workers for bounded follow-up packets. Worker names are handles, not permanent
specialties. The ledger must record actual source, worktree, writable paths,
read-only dependencies, runtime handles, exit gate, and next handoff.

Use the canonical [three execution lanes](#three-execution-lanes-bounded-work-in-progress) and [immediate sequence](#immediate-execution-sequence) above for current assignments; do not dispatch from historical resume tables.

Root assigns independent review to a worker who did not author the changed
algorithm. Rotate reviews as packets finish; do not add a fourth worker or make
root manually reproduce every mechanical validation. A worker is not alone in
the repository and must preserve others' changes. Each worker owns an isolated
worktree and cache; shared product artifacts are read-only.

Bound work in progress:

- One active implementation packet per worker, with at most one frozen handoff
  awaiting integration. A packet normally changes one shared primitive or a
  small coherent API family, not an entire namespace.
- Root keeps at most two new integration candidates waiting for review. Drain
  the existing frozen backlog before creating more competing controllers or
  cumulative products. A blocker is recorded with the exact missing dependency;
  the worker then takes an independent ready packet.
- Commit reviewable source and its scoped proof promptly. Merge and push a
  validated increment promptly on resume; performance-pending status may remain
  explicit after parity acceptance. Do not hold completed private kernels or
  tooling until unrelated algorithms finish.
- Prepare the next contract and timing cases during another packet's build or
  remote measurement. Keep builds/tests off the measurement host and never touch
  packages in use by the measurement.

## Waves and shared primitives

Waves describe dependency order, not whole-library barriers. An API may start
when its own dependencies are ready. The per-API roadmap assigns each key one
primary wave and records the actual prerequisites. Lane P starts early and runs
alongside the numerical work so data, I/O, and class state do not become a late
unplanned project.

| Wave | Deliverable and easiest route | Release criterion |
|---|---|---|
| W0 — current checkpoint closure | Reuse admitted252 evidence and line-AA scoped shipping; prepare the next coherent cumulative checkpoint without repeating closed campaigns | Exact-source supported-profile result and explicit performance dispositions; do not repeat historical 199 work. |
| W1 — cheap independent APIs and partial-contract closure | Existing native dtype/layout/keyword gaps; direct draw loops; util views/copies and conversions; basic statistics/moments; canonical alias reuse | Small public contracts with minimal new machinery, plus per-function timing. |
| W2 — neighborhood, ordering, connectivity | Reuse histogram/reduction/boundary primitives; rolling windows; reconstruction queue; stable union-find/tree traversal; rank and morphology extensions | Exact topology/tie/output-buffer behavior and safe boundary handling; dependencies shared by later families. |
| W3 — geometry, resampling, solvers, classes | Existing typed resampling/integral primitives; coordinate generation; C++ least-squares/SVD/QR where justified; common fitting/state layer | Full constructor/state/member behavior and actual solver paths, not class metadata alone. |
| W4 — FFT, multiscale, registration, restoration, features | One FFT/convolution layer, pyramids and gradients; direct C++ ports of feature/denoising loops; reuse stable eigensolvers | Fixed numerical policies, boundary/normalization correctness, actual one-worker provider control. |
| W5 — dependent graph, segmentation, model solvers | Graph construction/traversal over accepted labels and features; source-faithful watershed and optimization; reuse sparse/dense solver facilities | Exact discrete decisions plus full state/callback/randomness and convergence coverage. |
| P — providers, assets, I/O, class/state contracts | Asset manifest/cache and loader rules; codec adapters; collection laziness; plotting/UI adapters; external model protocols and runtime attributes | Exact API behavior and measured relevant work, explicit dependency/native frontier, no fake SIMD or image-kernel speed claim. |

Start from the current implementations rather than building a second foundation:

| Shared facility | Existing starting points | Ownership and extension rule |
|---|---|---|
| Typed array intake, strides, conversion and output ownership | `src/image.cpp`, `src/bind_numeric.hpp`, typed resampling bindings | One owned descriptor/boundary contract; extend it for a real family need, avoiding implicit copies that alter mutation or dtype semantics. |
| Global workers and CPU dispatch | `src/execution.hpp`, `src/image.cpp`, `src/simd.hpp`, existing `simd_*_avx2.cpp` | Preserve portable scalar operation and one worker default. Choose a workload-size threshold; prohibit nested provider oversubscription. |
| Histograms, reductions, ordering | `src/threshold.cpp`, `src/li.cpp`, `src/multiotsu.cpp`, `src/rank.cpp`, `src/metrics.cpp` | Reuse code where arithmetic/tie contracts match. Do not force numerically different reductions through one implementation merely to share code. |
| Neighborhoods and boundaries | `src/gaussian_nd.cpp`, `src/morphology.cpp`, `src/morphology_boolean.cpp`, footprints and rank | Share offsets/modes/halos after freezing their rules; keep feedback/output-alias traversals serial when required. |
| Connectivity and ordered queues | `src/components.cpp`, `src/component_consumers.cpp`, reviewed max-tree work, reconstruction draft | Stable labels and parents are public results. Parallelize independent preparation before changing dependent traversal. |
| Geometry and scale | `src/resample.cpp`, `src/resize_range.cpp`, `src/resize_clip.cpp`, `src/projective.cpp`, integral/downscale code | Compose native coordinates, sampling, and dtype policy; separate state/wrappers from the numerical solver. |
| FFT, sparse solvers, codecs and assets | New narrowly scoped facilities only when a roadmap family needs them | Compare viable libraries against the pinned contract; do not create a general framework before the first consumer. |

SIMD applies to independent pixels, coordinates, scans, arithmetic, and selected
reduction stages. Parallelism applies to independent rows, tiles, channels, or
preparation work after a measured size threshold. Tree/queue dependencies,
Gauss-Seidel updates, callback order, stable tie reduction, lazy I/O state, and
metadata often require serial execution. They still honor the global worker
policy, but forcing a parallel algorithm is not an acceptance criterion.

## Qualification, evidence reuse, and early measurement

```mermaid
flowchart LR
    A[Per-API contract and numerical policy] --> K[Small native kernel or composition]
    A --> H[Original cases and timing corpus]
    S[Frozen shared interface] --> K
    K --> P[Actual installed family parity and SDK]
    P --> Q[Exact cumulative product qualification]
    H --> M[Isolated original vs native measurement]
    P --> M
    Q --> RLS[Whole-product release acceptance]
    M --> R[Retained regression and bottleneck packet]
    R --> K
```

Keep four distinct gates:

1. **Reference gate:** original source and safe original calls establish the
   contract. Metadata checks, expected failures, and unsafe blocked inputs are
   reported separately from successful numerical calls.
2. **Native family gate:** execute the real C++ binding and installed interface
   with fallback disabled. Check the changed family, dependent shared primitives,
   scalar/SIMD modes, default-one/optional workers, error/state behavior, and
   appropriate native/sanitizer/portable cases. A temporary adapter is private
   evidence only.
   Before the broad campaign, reuse the relevant schema/checkout regressions and
   run one ordinary owned-array call through each changed binding. Check the
   required cohort identities, not only a nonempty green XML or total count.
3. **Public product gate:** freeze source, wheel, SDK, dependency versions and
   build/runtime identity; run the existing genuine four-profile whole-product
   qualification for the actual cumulative candidate. The family gate catches
   defects early; it does not replace this release gate. Unaffected private
   evidence may be referenced only with explicit identical dependency closure.
4. **Performance gate:** consume genuine prepared, installed-family-validated artifacts and original
   0.26.0 in isolated one-worker roles. Verify the frozen corpus first, then time
   complete public calls. Retain every case, sample, regression and failed trial.

Use one reusable proof/measurement format and the existing qualification tools
for future families. A family supplies cases, comparisons, expected scope, and
commands; it should not need a new controller, retainer, publisher, and bespoke
certificate for each API. Any tool consolidation is a separate future packet:
preserve historical controllers and raw bytes, and validate the shared tool on
known success, failure, and tampered-input fixtures before adopting it.

A handoff needs source and owned-path deltas, full dependency fingerprints,
commands/results, actual artifact hashes, native/reference/provider/metadata
case counts, unresolved cases, process handles, and one independent verifier
receipt. Root reviews that bounded packet and its exceptions. Reuse an existing
verified immutable object by content hash and exact source/dependency scope;
never infer current behavior from a historical test count or relabel a product.
Fresh changed-source execution remains necessary. Do not repeatedly copy and
manually replay unchanged archives solely to create a new administrative seal.
State the changed inputs and reused receipt/closure explicitly in the existing
handoff. Do not add a separate manifest or controller just to repeat that split.
Name the next missing gate, owner and public/SDK integration destination in each
private family's existing packet. For future footprint observations, reuse the
colocalization node-to-raw-file mapping so worker identity is independently
recoverable. Preserve current evidence without inventing per-row labels.

Prepare performance corpora with the contract, and measure each newly qualified
family promptly. Existing comparisons already identify useful work: metrics,
large multivalued/high-connectivity labeling, footprint decomposition, rank
windowed histograms/noise-filter cases, and TV raster/volume cases. Prioritize
absolute time and representative workload impact, not only large speedup ratios
on tiny calls. Keep allocation/conversion/setup costs visible. Asset/I/O APIs
need reproducible fixture and cache-state measurements; metadata and state-only
APIs need relevant latency/behavior checks, not invented image throughput.
At the next qualified function, update its existing performance record with the
corpus, qualified product, measurement or precise blocker, worst retained
absolute regression, and next owner. After the current review, reserve one
execution lane for a measured bottleneck; choose its case from retained absolute
costs rather than a geometric mean. Missing absolute-cost data is a reporting
gap, not a reason to guess the most important case or claim it optimized.

No local timing experiments. Hosted timing uses one native worker and one
original provider thread, matched inputs, repeated paired rounds, and recorded
runtime/CPU identities. Additional worker counts are supplementary. Do not time
alongside builds/tests, rebuild a package being timed, discard adverse cases, or
claim a later source inherits an earlier speedup. A measured baseline with
regressions is progress, not completion of the optimization objective.

## Completion tracking and operations

Report progress by evidence rather than assertion totals:

- 477 public keys: contract assessed / native partial / full advertised native
  contract passed / measured / performance target met; split aliases, metadata,
  assets, and providers from canonical computational algorithms.
- 29 classes and all 284 static member paths: constructor/state/member/solver
  status, plus runtime-discovered fields and namespaces still to assess.
- Per family: tested dtype/layout/parameter partitions, native cases versus
  reference/provider/metadata cases, unsupported partitions, and retained
  regressions with absolute cost and next owner.
- Queue health: active owners and writable paths, frozen handoff age, blockers,
  exact live handles, next merge, and clean obsolete worktrees eligible for
  removal. These operational measures are not implementation acceptance.

The goal is complete only when every inventoried and newly discovered in-scope
public contract is accounted for, required computation is genuinely native,
per-function and class/state parity passes, installed C++/Python products are
qualified, and original-library one-worker evidence establishes the declared
performance result for each relevant API. Do not mark the goal complete with
unsupported argument partitions, hidden algorithm providers, unmeasured APIs,
or unresolved optimization targets disguised by aggregate averages.

These commands inspect the queue; they do not resume the goal or launch work:

```powershell
.venv/Scripts/python.exe tools/work_queue.py --check
.venv/Scripts/python.exe tools/work_queue.py --check --verify-local-proofs
.venv/Scripts/python.exe tools/work_queue.py --agents
.venv/Scripts/python.exe tools/work_queue.py --ready
.venv/Scripts/python.exe tools/work_queue.py --packet <reviewed-packet-id>
.venv/Scripts/python.exe tools/generate_api_implementation_paths.py --check
```

The checker catches cycles, malformed proofs, ownership conflicts and inventory
accounting. It does not prove that a packet's state is current, that an agent is
live, that a proof covers all arguments, or that a performance target was met.
Before dispatch, root reconciles old packet states with actual receipts and
the per-API roadmap; ready does not mean unfinished. Every task closeout includes
the waste review above before its next dependent task is selected.

Follow [AGENTS.md](../AGENTS.md) after each validated merge. Remove an obsolete
worktree only with `git worktree remove`, after confirming its commits are
ancestors of main and its tracked/untracked source work is clean. Retain active
environments, running-job files, exact baseline/qualified products, raw results,
rejected trials, and unfinished source. Resolve every cleanup path inside the
workspace before deletion; never delete the whole `build/` directory. Existing
cleanup rejection records stay explicit. No cleanup is authorized by this
planning revision.
